How To Beat The Fire Temple In The Ocarina Of Time Remake

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time remake is finally here, so shove a fairy in a bottle, get your Iron Boots, and head out to slay all types of monsters. Throughout this adventure, you will be tasked with completing several thematic dungeons all over Hyrule.

One of these is the Fire Temple, which is the second dungeon you'll complete as an adult. This one can be a bit tricky thanks to the mix of dangerous enemies, traps, and fiery puzzles. So if you're struggling to complete this dungeon or just want to ensure you find all the Gold Skulltulas inside, here is a complete walkthrough of the Fire Temple.

Upon entering the Fire Temple, you'll be met by a flight of stairs and two Fire Keese near the back. Go up the stairs, kill the bats, and use the door on the left. This will trigger a brief cutscene with Darunia, who explains that Ganondorf unleashed the dragon Volvagia and is going to feed the Gorons to it. He asks you to save all ten of them and then goes charging in to fight the beast.

Dark Link is even more of an aura farmer than ever before in the Ocarina of Time remake.

Your first Goron that needs rescuing is conveniently in the same room. After speaking with Daruina, look to the left and jump across the platforms until you reach the back left corner. Be careful, as falling in will ensure you take damage due to the lava. Once you reach the back left corner, go into the adjoining side chamber and step on the stone switch.

This will open the bars to the jail, freeing the Goron and triggering a small dialogue exchange. He will explain that you can reach Volvagia's room if you can dislodge the massive stalactite in the ceiling. We're a long way from doing that, so make a mental note of it and then open the chest he was clutching to get your first Small Key.

Now that we have a Small Key, return to the entrance room of the Fire Temple and go through the door at the top of the stairs that's locked. This will bring you to a large chamber with several Fire Keese on the right and a couple Red Bubbles lurking in the fire. The latter act like the Bubbles in the Forest Temple, but will emerge from the lava. Use your shield to block their charge and then slash at them to destroy this foe.

In this room, make your way left across the platforms. Be careful of the large square-shaped ones, as they will slowly sink into the lava due to your weight. Quickly run across them to ensure you don't fall into the lava below! You'll also be attacked by a Red Bubble as you cross, so just ignore it and reach solid ground on the left side of the room.

Once you cross the lava, you'll find another small jail cell with a Goron inside it. Step on the switch to raise the bars, talk to the Goron, and open the chest to get another Small Key.

Staying on the left side, go up the side and jump onto the large square platform in the back left corner of the room. Instead of sinking, this one will raise you to the next floor. Step up to where the blue Time Block is and play the Song of Time on the ocarina. This will teleport the block down to the marker in front of you, creating a makeshift staircase you can climb!

Go through the door to be introduced to two new enemies. The first is the Flying Tiles, which are just floor pieces that became sentient and decided to choose violence. You can easily defeat them by just blocking their attack with your shield or outright avoiding them. It's the other enemy that's infinitely more dangerous.

The massive leech in the back is a Like Like, which will attempt to swallow you whole and strip you of key gear pieces until it's killed. To defeat this, wait until this foe leans forward and then dodge to the left or right. This will ensure it misses its attempt to eat you while leaving itself open to being attacked.

Once you've defeated both the Flying Tiles and Like Like, you can find the Gold Skulltula on the wall at the back of the room.

Now that you have the Gold Skulltula, go back to the large cavern and head to the opposite side of the room. Watch out for the Red Bubbles and Fire Keese; they will attack without warning while you're jumping across the platforms. Once you get to the right side, use a bomb to blow up the grey-colored, cracked wall.

This will reveal another jail cell, with a stone switch you can step on. Just like before, free the Goron, talk to him, and then open the chest to get another Small Key. That's all we're doing in this room, so head to the only locked door along the back when you're ready to continue.

Use the first Small Key to unlock the door in the cavern, and then use your second Small Key on the locked door along the back wall. Ignore the jet of fire for now and go into the newly unlocked room. To your left and right will be metal chains that you can actually climb like vines or netting.

Climb up the chains to reach the top of the flame jet room. Go over to the stone block and push it into the gap once the fire jet stops spewing flames. You can then hop onto the stone block before the fire ignites and ride it up to the next floor above you.

Step off the makeshift elevator once you reach the room with all the Flame Slugs. These enemies are more annoying than dangerous, as hitting them once with your sword will cause them to flee around the room. Chase after and kill all three Flame Slugs in the room before climbing up where the gem switch is.

Equip the Fairy Bow and then pull the stone block that's hiding in the corner out of the wall. Move it in front of the jets of flame and climb onto the block. Turn around and shoot the gem switch with your bow, then climb up two sets of stairs. Turn back to the gem switch and hit it with a second arrow to clear the path.

Climb the chain wall to enter the boulder maze. This area is filled with large rolling rocks that follow set paths on the ground. You can tell the path of a rock by the indentations on the ground, which allows you to know where it is safe to stand and when you should move.

You can find the second Gold Skulltula in the boulder maze. From the entrance, head down the slope and go right to the north side of the room. Along the corner on the northeast side of the room, you will find a bombable wall. Drop an explosive by it to blow open and reveal a Gold Skulltula behind it.

The fourth Goron we need to save can be found in the northernmost part of the boulder maze. From the ramp you enter, go right and make your way through the maze. Near the northwest corner, there will be a small opening with a stone switch on it. Step on the switch to raise the bars, freeing the Goron. Open up the chest he was holding to get a Small Key.

Now that we have the Small Key, head to the south side of the boulder maze. In the southwest corner, there will be a straight hallway that leads right to a large chest. Open the chest to get the Dungeon Map. Once you have the map, go back north, hugging the wall on the left side of the room. You will find a locked door, which you can use your Small Key on.

Conveniently, the Compass is also close by. Upon unlocking the door, you will enter a connecting chamber where most of the floor has collapsed and fallen into the lava below. Slowly walk across the narrow path and look for an eye switch over a barred door. Take out the Fairy Bow and shoot the switch, unlocking the door.

Go through the door, and to the right you will find a large chest with the Compass inside. If only all of the collectibles in this game were so easy!

After getting the Compass, head back to the narrow path room and go through the other door you haven't used yet. You'll see a large series of metal platforms suspended over lava. Hop onto the closest one and move a few feet forward to trigger a wall of flames to spawn behind Link. This flame wall will begin moving through the room, chasing you as you run and jump across the meta platforms.

The moment you regain control of Link, quickly move across the different metal platforms until you reach the stone wall on the other side. Be careful, as there will be numerous Red Bubbles that appear throughout the lava. Do not stop to attack them; instead, ignore these enemies and just head for safety. They aren't worth the trouble.

Once you're safe from the wall of fire, approach the stone block in the wall and push it into the boulder room. This will give you a shortcut to the boulder maze's lower floor.

Gold Skulltula 3 — Scarecrow Song Room (4F)

Before we save the Goron, we can actually get two Gold Skulltulas and quite a bit of extra Rupees. Once you push the block down, jump onto the platform with the Flame Slug and kill it. Look up the wall you just came through and you'll see a Scarecrow spawn point. Play the Scarecrow Song to summon him and use your Hookshot to pull yourself up. Then grapple to the small elevator directly across from the Scarecrow. In the next room at the very top there will be a Gold Skulltula on the wall.

Gold Skulltula 4 — Treasure Chest Room (5F)

Directly after getting the previous Gold Skulltula, go through the door and look to the right. You will see the spider on the wall, which you can kill and snag the token with the Hookshot. Additionally, stepping on the nearby stone switch will activate two pillars that slowly lower into the ground. Use the Hookshot to make your way around the room to the small chests, which contain Purple and Red Rupees. When you're ready, exit out the way you came and return to the boulder room.

Now, head to the southwest corner of the Boulder Maze, making sure to not fall down to the ground floor with all the rolling rocks. There you will find another Goron behind bars that you can free by stepping on the nearby stone switch. Once he's safe, open the chest to get a Small Key.

To reach the next Goron, move across the second floor of the Boulder Room and approach the large, cracked rocky mound along the east side. Place a bomb in the conveniently bomb-shaped divot and jump down the hole that the explosion creates. Down there, you will immediately find a stone switch you can step on, which frees the next Goron. Talk to him and then open the chest for a Small Key.

This will also open up a shortcut to the gem switch room you went through earlier. Head back up to the boulder room and return to where you got chased by the wall of fire. Along the west side of the room, there will be a locked door that you can reach by jumping from the closest metal platform (shown above). Wait for the flame wall to pass, and then run and jump to reach this locked door.

I'm going to be real, this next room is annoying. Welcome to the flame maze, which not only has Fire Keese circling, but rolling boulders you'll need to contend with. As the namesake suggests, the gimmick of this room is that there are several dozen poles in the ground, which will (or will not) summon a wall of fire between them when you're close.

First, head south and make your way through the fire maze to the locked door that connects to the hall in the middle. Be careful; along with the Fire Keese, there will be rotting jets of flames and boulders, so time your movements carefully. Additionally, you will want to walk slowly through the maze, as the walls of fire will erupt when you're near them. This can save you the headache of accidentally running into them. In the photo above, the route you want to take is colored red.

Be careful, there are several fake doors along the walls in this room. If you see the door move or shift at all, you can destroy it with a bomb. It will only attack if you try to open it.

Go through the hall and exit the door at the other end. This will spit you out on the north side of the maze. Now follow the green colored path in the photo above to reach the two small stone blocks in the northeast corner of the maze. Grab one of the blocks and backtrack to where you exited the door. Now, follow the blue route to the stone switch in the middle of the maze. Set the block down onto the switch to stop all the flames on this side of the maze.

With all the fire turned off, make your way northeast to find a previously inaccessible hallway with a Goron cell in it. Step on the stone switch to open the cell, then speak to the Goron, and open the chest to get a Small Key. Now that we have a key,

Go to the locked door on the northwest side of the room and enter. Beforehand, make sure to hit any pots around the now inactive flame maze because you're in for a mini-boss fight on the other side of that door. When you enter, a brief cutscene will trigger as the Flare Dancer mini-boss spawns.

When the fight begins, the Flare Dancer will begin skating in a circle around the arena. When it gets close, the Flare Dancer will spin like a top and launch itself at you. Unfortunately, in this state, the Flare Dancer will be basically immune to all damage from all weapons. The only exception is the Hookshot, but due to the speed, it can be difficult to strike this mini-boss.

Instead, wait for the Flare Dancer to start spinning towards you and then launch the Hookshot at its chest piece. This will extract the real Flare Dancer from the flaming body, which then proceeds to... run away like a coward. Great. The Flare Dancer is fast, so run in the opposite direction of this little fella is moving (either counterclockwise or clockwise).

Once you two meet, slash at it with your sword a few times before it runs the other direction. Repeat this a couple of times before the Flare Dancer jumps into the flames to restore its body. This will also cause small fireballs to bounce around sections of the arena. Wait until the mini-boss attempts to ram itself into you again before ripping the body from the fire.

Keep repeating this process of ripping the boss out of the fire and attacking it while it tries to run away until this mini-boss is vanquished.

With the Flare Dancer defeated, head onto the makeshift life that is created where the mini-boss used to restore its flames. Ride this up to the next room and climb up to the endless wall of fire. To the left, you will find another gem switch, so use your Hookshot to hit it and turn the flames off.

Now, go past the now inactive jets of fire, equip the bombs, and throw a bomb while remaining behind the inactive jets at the gem switch. The explosion will turn the fire you just crossed back on, while shutting off the flame jets behind you. Go into the next room, which is where you will find the Megaton Hammer.

When you enter this room, before stepping on the switch, take out your bow and use it to kill the three Fire Keese. Once they are dead, step on the switch to raise a staircase and follow it up to the raised section with the large chest. Open the chest to obtain a new weapon called the Megaton Hammer.

This item is a slow-moving, hard-hitting weapon that lets you not only flatten enemies, but also break a variety of previously impervious objects. Don't worry, you're about to go through a brief tutorial on how to use this weapon.

First, make your way back to the stone switch and look for a large, metal panel on the ground with the Megaton Hammer symbol on it. Hitting the panel will cause it to fall through the floor, giving you access to the floor below.

In the next room, you can use the Megaton Hammer to flatten and instantly kill the Flame Slug. Then use the hammer to break apart the statue blocking the door. Go through the door, kill all the Fire Keese in the room, and then strike the circular metal switch with the Megaton Hammer to form a staircase leading downstairs.

Hit the next metal panel on the floor and then drop through the hole into the small hallway. Now use your hammer to shatter the large grey rocks, before entering the room with the Time Block. Approach the blue block, pull out your Ocarina of Time, and play the Song of Time to cause it to vanish, opening up the path forward.

Now hit the metal switch to unlock the jail cell the Goron is in. Speak with him and then open the chest to get the Small Key Only one Goron left to save!

With the ninth Goron saved, hit the circular metal panel behind the chest to drop a massive stalactite down where the Boss Room Entrance is. Now, head back to the entrance room, go down the stairs and to the right will be another statue you can break apart with your Megaton Hammer. Use the Small Key you got from the Goron to unlock the door.

In the next room, there will be a ton of Flame Slugs. Use the hammer to flatten and kill all of them to unlock the next door. More Flying Tiles and a Like Like will greet you when you enter. Use your shield to block the tiles, before carefully dealing with the Like Like. When you're ready to proceed, destroy the statue blocking the door on the left.

Golden Skulltula 5 — Flying Tile Room 2 (1F)

In the second Flying Tile Room, you will find a large grey rock along the back wall. Use the Megaton Hammer to break the rock, revealing the Gold Skulltula right behind it.

Upon entering the next room, you will be locked inside, and the Flare Dancer will make another appearance. However, this time you can trivialize the fight thanks to the Megaton Hammer. Once he starts skating around the room, get close and smack the ground with the hammer. The shockwave will knock the Flar Dancer off its feet, destryoing its flame body.

Just like before, run the opposite direction of the Flare Dancer is retreating to catch it and allow you to get a few sword strikes in. This fight basically plays out like the previous one, with the only change being the Flare Dancer will now summon more fireballs that bounce around the room once it restores its body.

With the Flare Dancer defeated, go through the unlocked door and use the Megaton Hammer to hit the metal switch. This will unlock the cage the Goron is in, freeing him and giving you access to the Boss Key. Now return to the Boss Room entrance, equip the hammer, and go inside.

You can find a Fairy to put in an Empty Bottle by using the grapple point to the right of the Boss Room. Climb up the blocks and there will be four jars containing some ammo and a Fairy to capture.

Compared to all the previous bosses you've faced, Volvagia is far and away the most dangerous. This slender dragon can deal some serious damage and has some wide-ranging attacks. Because of this, I do recommend having at least a Red Potion handy just in case you need to quickly refill your health in the middle of the battle.

When the fight starts, Volvagia will go into one of the many fiery holes scattered throughout the arena. Pull out your Megaton Hammer and wait for one of them to start bubbling. This will indicate that Volvagia is going to erupt from that pit and will swing its fiery hair in front of him when he emerges.

Keep your distance when Volvagia appears and wait for him to start charging his fire breath attack. Run to the side and hit his head with the Megaton Hammer, shattering the rock on his skull. This will stun Volvagia, allowing you to slash at his head for a few seconds before he retreats. He will then repeat this a second time, before flying up into the skies.

When Volvagia is flying around, keep an eye on him, as he will soon release a line of fire in front of him as he passes over the arena. This both does quite a lot of damage if you're hit and will leave behind lingering flames that last on the arena floor for several seconds. Volvagia will then retreat back into the arena floor.

Now it's time to play whack-a-dragon, as the various fire holes will begin to bubble. Don't just run over to the first one, as the dragon will not emerge from the first few that look like he's about to rise from. Stay near the arena edge and wait until Volvagia actually commits and emerges from the fire.

Then run over to him like before and smack him with the hammer to stun the boss. After taking more damage from your sword attacks, Volvagia will take offense to the multiple concussions you're giving him and begin raining down fiery boulders from the ceiling. Watch for their shadows on the ground and avoid them the best you can. Don't bother looking at Volvagia during this move, just focus on the ground and watch for shadows. There are a lot of falling rocks, so it can be tricky to dodge them all at once.

Volvagia will then go back into the lava, so wait for him to emerge, hit him with a hammer, and damage him with your sword before he recovers. The boss will fly around the arena once more, releasing multiple lines of fire which can make moving around the arena a little tricky. I once again cannot emphasize enough how much damage some of his moves do, so it's much better to be cautious in this fight.

Appeals Court Says Lawsuit Over ‘The Pitt’ Can Proceed, Cites ‘Numerous’ Similarities to ‘ER’

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

A California appeals court ruled Wednesday that the Emmy-winning medical drama The Pitt shares “clear and numerous” similarities with Michael Crichton’s 1990s TV hit ER, handing Crichton’s estate a critical victory in its legal battle with Warner Bros. Television.

In a 33-page opinion, a three-judge panel unanimously upheld a lower court’s ruling that Crichton’s estate had presented enough evidence to pursue its claim that The Pitt is an unauthorized reboot of ER. Warner Bros. sought to toss the lawsuit on appeal, arguing the shows shared only “generic similarities” common to “any dramatic story involving emergency room doctors.”

The justices with the Second District Court of Appeal said they compared the pilot scripts for both shows and found sufficient similarities in terms of plot, themes, dialogue, mood, settings, pacing, characters, and sequence of events to allow the lawsuit to proceed at this early stage.

“Both shows take place, with little exception, in the emergency room, as opposed to other medical shows which track patients as they move out of the emergency room, or indeed never enter it. The pacing of events is also common: very fast pacing, and reactions to multiple overlapping crises,” the justices said in their opinion obtained by Rolling Stone.

“The fast-paced dialogue is (to a layperson) frequently incomprehensible shorthand and jargon, lending an air of immediacy and accuracy to the presentation,” they wrote. “Both shows feature a series of patients: they are presented in overlapping sequences, with medical personnel interrupting their work with one to attend to another, layering on crisis after crisis.”

The two shows also “display graphic depictions of injuries,” and each “includes a young doctor, fresh from medical school and incompetent.” This is evidence that the plot, themes, dialogue, mood, setting, pace, characters, and sequence of events in two works are similar enough to warrant a possible trial, they said. Editor’s picks The 250 Greatest Albums of the 21st Century So Far The 100 Best TV Episodes of All Time The 500 Greatest Albums of All Time 100 Best Movies of the 21st Century

A lawyer for Warner Bros. and the producers did not immediately respond to a request for comment.

The underlying lawsuit was first filed in August 2024 by Crichton’s widow, Sherri Crichton, against defendants Warner Bros., producer John Wells, and actor-producer Noah Wyle. It alleged that when Crichton sold Warner Bros. the screenplay for what became the pilot episode of ER, Warner Bros. promised that no subsequent productions derived from ER would go forward without Crichton’s consent. It claims that after negotiating unsuccessfully with Crichton’s estate for nearly a year for the right to reboot ER, Warner Bros. “simply moved the show from Chicago to Pittsburgh, rebranded it The Pitt, and has plowed ahead without any attribution or compensation for Crichton and his heirs.”

In their ruling Wednesday, the appeals court justices dismissed separate claims against Wyle, Wells, and producer-showrunner R. Scott Gemmill, saying there was insufficient evidence to show the producers were aware they could be violating Crichton’s contractual rights.

“I am deeply gratified by the Court of Appeal decision and look forward to discovery and to our case, which affects writers, creators, and their families and estates, being presented to a jury,” Sherri Crichton said in a statement shared by her lawyer on Wednesday.

Warner Bros., Wells, and Wyle denied the allegations in the lawsuit and sought to dismiss the case on the basis that it was frivolous and an attempt to violate their rights to free speech. Los Angeles County Superior Court Judge Wendy Chang was the first to reject their challenge, known as an anti-SLAPP, saying the estate’s complaint met the “minimal merit standard” to proceed. Wyle, who starred in ER alongside Anthony Edwards, George Clooney, and Julianna Margulies, plays the lead character in The Pitt. The show’s producer, Wells, was the original ER showrunner.

In a sworn statement, Wyle denied the new show is an unauthorized offshoot of the Crichton hit. “I do not see how The Pitt could be deemed derivative of ER because they are two entirely different shows,” he said. “ER and The Pitt do not have any of the same characters. Dr. Carter from ER was a blue blood, wealthy, and highly educated doctor. He came from a waspy background. Dr. Robinavitch comes from a blue-collar background, he is Jewish, did his residency in New Orleans, and is recovering from PTSD,” Wyle said.

The next hearing in the case is set for Jan. 21, back in Judge Chang’s courtroom in downtown Los Angeles.

Gamers Love Ad Blockers. So This Site Found Another Way To Show Them Ads

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Ad blocking doesn’t just prevent publishers from earning revenue; it also creates blind spots in their audience analytics that can affect everything from monetization strategy to editorial coverage.

Gamers are very likely to use ad blockers, which has led to significant challenges for WiseGuyGaming, a video game-focused publication that launched last year.

Its co-founder and CEO, Mike Smith, launched the publication with his son Mikhail following previous stints handling audience data and monetization for HBO, Forbes and Hearst. After two-plus decades working at established media brands, he wanted to start his own, he told AdExchanger.

However, the elder Smith quickly realized that the audience he most wanted to reach – technologically sophisticated gamers – are disproportionately likely to use the most advanced ad blockers on the market. Those ad blockers made this audience largely invisible to existing analytics solutions, in addition to being unreachable with banner and video ads.

But, fortunately, high-end ad blockers mimic some of the same online behavior as bot traffic. So Smith worked with Don Marti, a consultant specializing in open-source software, digital privacy and ad tech, to repurpose the tools WiseGuyGaming was already using to identify bot crawlers to reveal its ad-blocking audience as well.

As a result, WiseGuyGaming found that about 18% of its audience used ad-blocking tech. Now it’s strategizing how to reach that audience in ways that can’t be blocked as easily as a web banner.

In addition to the Google Analytics tools widely used by publishers, WiseGuyGaming also relies on another popular third-party audience analytics and bot filtration tool, FouAnalytics, to measure its audience.

It’s especially important for publishers to use multiple analytics solutions these days, Marti said, because an explosion in bot-crawling activity to support generative and agentic AI has made it harder to separate human site visitors from bots.

Plus, Marti said, there are now more services like Bright Data that turn consumer devices like cellphones and TVs into residential proxies for AI scraping and other bot activity.

Basically, if an app installed on a person’s TV partners with Bright Data, that person can opt in to allow their IP address to be used to mask bot behavior. In exchange, they get an ad-free experience when using the app or some other incentive.

These proxy services make it harder for publishers to weed out bots using traditional methods like blocking IP addresses associated with previous bot activity, Marti said. Proxy services can make a bot appear to be using a residential IP address, rather than one tied to a data center.

However, there are nuances in how bot crawlers work when they load a publisher page that can be exploited to detect them, Marti said.

For instance, he said, many crawlers are configured to scrape a website’s data before any third-party scripts – including ad tech and bot-blocking tech – are able to execute on the page.

“The crawling company has been told, ‘Go to this page and [extract] the content of the big table on that page as JSON,’” Marti said. “The crawler is smart enough to know that all the social widgets on the page, all the random third-party JavaScript, ads, analytics – it doesn’t need that for the mission it’s on.” The crawler’s headless browser often doesn’t even bother sending the network requests required for the third-party scripts to run, he said.

But, as it turns out, Marti added, ad blockers likewise prevent the browser from sending network requests to ping third-party scripts, including ad tech and analytics. Which means publishers can use more advanced bot-detection strategies to also reveal when a human site visitor is using an ad blocker.

When a user with an ad blocker arrives on the page, the ad blocker references lists of known third-party ad servers and domains associated with ad tech, then prevents network requests from pinging those servers and domains.

Two of the lists most widely used by ad blockers are called EasyList and EasyPrivacy, Marti said. The really dedicated ad blockers that serious gamers and tech heads use, such as uBlock Origin, use both EasyPrivacy and EasyList, he said.

EasyPrivacy has FouAnalytics on its list of domains to block, Marti said. “So anyone who’s running FouAnalytics normally is going to miss the EasyPrivacy users because the FouAnalytics script is blocked before it even has a chance to load.”

Marti and Smith’s solution was to set up two versions of FouAnalytics on the WiseGuyGaming site.

One version worked as normal, Marti said, loading on the site as third-party analytics JavaScript that would be blocked by EasyPrivacy-equipped ad blockers.

But the other version used a “proxy pass” – or a first-party URL – to obscure the third-party script, he said. “It’s on wiseguygaming.com, so it’s not going to show up on any of the filter lists. But it is proxied through to the same FouAnalytics server as the third-party script, so we’ve got the exact same script being served onto the page twice.”

WiseGuyGaming could then compare data between its two different FouAnalytics dashboards to see which users were blocking the FouAnalytics third-party script, but not blocking FouAnalytics when it was using the proxy pass.

And since both Google Analytics and FouAnalytics had already prefiltered out likely bots, WiseGuyGaming could conclude with reasonable certainty that the remaining 18% still blocking FouAnalytics as a third-party script were probably either invalid traffic that evaded the bot filter or humans using an ad blocker.

Once WiseGuyGaming nailed down that roughly 18% figure for its ad-blocking audience, it then had to come up with a new way to reach those users, since banner ads wouldn’t cut it.

WiseGuyGaming’s immediate solution was to produce more sponsored and native content, which ad blockers are not configured to recognize, Smith said.

Meanwhile, the site’s editorial team was able to analyze the ad-blocking audience’s traffic patterns to understand what content was resonating with them, Smith said. Which is helping the editors to publish more content that can bring in that highly technical audience the Smiths want to reach.

The solution did have one flaw, Marti noted. It required them to look at two different FouAnalytics dashboards in two different browser tabs to compare the ad-blocking and non-ad-blocking audiences.

But that’s a minor concern, since the solution has transformed WiseGuyGaming’s editorial and monetization strategies, Smith said.

“We set out first to get a very pure count of our audience,” he said. “We are now going to launch a paid subscription service and use email and push [notification] technology to reach the roughly 18% that we found.”

Criteo has weathered considerable storms in its day, including a painful moment in March 2020 when its market cap bottomed out below $500 million. Can it pull off yet another turnaround?

Disaffection and outright anger toward US sports institutions run deeper than logo-cluttered fields. Leagues and teams are sacrificing player safety and fan enjoyment for marketing dollars.

People Inc.’s referral traffic from Google search is down 70% over the past two years, and by 26% in Q3 alone. But its digital revenue is still growing, albeit slowly.

On Tuesday, Chord, a six-year-old commerce tech startup, acquired Zenlytic, an AI startup with an anthropomorphized data analyst agent named “Zoë,” for $5 million.

If you ask the executive leadership of Affle and Perion why the two companies are merging, the answer you’ll get is “synergies.”

A combo of Tubi, sports and political ads powered Fox’s growth this last quarter, even as Fox News lost viewers and the Roku deal awaits DOJ clearance.

AdExchanger is where marketers, agencies, publishers and tech companies go for the latest information on the trends that are transforming digital media and marketing, from data, privacy, identity and AI to commerce, CTV, measurement and mobile.

Where To Find All The Big Poes In The Ocarina Of Time Remake

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time remake is here, so it's time to once again save Hyrule from the evil Ganondorf and his army of monsters. While you will spend the vast majority of your time completing dungeons and progressing the main story, there are a few side quests you can complete.

One of these requires you to hunt down ten Big Poes in Hyrule Field and then sell them to the Poe Collector who has taken up refuge in Hyrule Castle Town. If you're looking to complete this quest, here is where to find all of the Big Poes and what you get for locating all of them.

Before we get into the specific locations, it's critical to note that this quest can only be completed once you've finished The Forest Temple and unlocked Epona. This is because the Fairy Bow is key to defeating the Big Poes, and you'll need Epona to actually summon them in Hyrule Fields. You will also need at least one Empty Bottle, although ideally you have four of the five, so you can save on backtracking.

Once you're ready, go to Hyrule Castle Town and talk to the Poe Collector. They will explain that they're looking to purchase Poes from you and will ask that you find all ten in Hyrule Field. To do this, summon Epona and look for low stone walls that are surrounded by a dark purple mist. Ride Epona towards the stone wall and jump over it by using a carrot to obtain a speed boost.

Successfully vaulting the Poe-infested wall will summon the Big Poe, which will begin running away. Equip your Fairy Bow and chase after it with Epona. You now need to hit the Big Poe with two arrows before it can escape. This is easier said than done, as the Big Poe is quick, and you'll need to fire while riding Epona.

Upon killing a Big Poe, it will drop a gold-colored soul, which can be stored in a bottle. Now take the soul back to the Poe Collector and sell it to them. This is the full loop you'll need to go through ten times in order to complete this side quest. Thankfully, having more Bottles reduces the amount of time you'll spend going to and from the Poe Collector.

The first Big Poe can be found right outside of Hyrule Castle, directly on the road going to Lon Lon Ranch. It will be right behind stone Hyrule sign. Jump over the wall to spawn the Big Poe and then chase it down.

Big Poe number two is directly to the right when you exit Hyrule Castle. It will be along the moat, making it quite easy to locate.

Your next Big Poe is located a little farther up the road to Lon Lon Ranch. Near the entrance to the ranch, there will be a fence that has the glowing purple lantern and mist emanating from it. Jump over the fence to spawn the Big Poe.

Back at Lon Lon Ranch, on the east side, you will find a large overturned tree trunk that is glowing with ghostly energy. This one can be a little tricky to vault due to the limited space, so take your time lining it up before riding Epona at it. I also recommend coming from the south side, as this will make the Poe easier to chase.

Your fifth Big Poe can be found outside of Gerudo Valley to the east. It will be right by the incline that leads up to the main entrance. Look for a short stone wall that is glowing. Be careful though, as there is a huge flock of enemy birds nearby that can make actually putting the Big Poe in the bottle quite dangerous.

The sixth Big Poe is between Lon Lon Ranch and Gerudo Valley. Located on a hill just east of the ranch, you can find this corrupted, overturned tree by a massive rock. It will also be right under that large flock of hostile birds, so try to kill the Big Poe a little bit away from them so you're not swarmed.

For this Big Poe, head directly south of Lon Lon Ranch to where the forest filled with Deku Scrubs is. Near the fence of Lon Lon Ranch will be another ghostly tree branch with a lantern hanging off it. Jump over the branch to summon the Big Poe.

The next Big Poe can be found to the east of the Lake Hylia entrance near the wetlands. Look for another overturned tree to jump over and spawn your next target.

Big Poe number nine is a bit of a pain to get, as chasing it north towards Kokiri Forest may result in it getting away. This is due to the small river having quite a few sections that Epona refuses to jump. Instead, come at this section from the north, ensuring you have a ton of space to chase after this ghost.

Finally, the last Big Poe can be found just west of where Poe nine was. Look for a long stone wall on the hill, and at the end will be a broken section with a lantern on it. Jump over the lantern section of the wall to spawn the last Big Poe.

As for rewards, along with the 30 Rupees you get per Big Poe, you'll also earn 100 points with the Poe Collector. Getting a total of 500 points (Selling five Big Poes) will net you an Empty Bottle! For those willing to hunt down all ten Big Poes, the collector will give you a Gold Rupee, which is a whopping 200 Rupees. This can be quite nice if you need to buy Magic Beans or some of the tunics for the different dungeons!

We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.

How To Beat The Forest Temple In The Ocarina Of Time Remake

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

There are a lot of dangerous places to explore in The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time. From Death Mountain to Kakariko Graveyard to inside a giant fish, you'll venture into all manner of thematic locations.

One of my personal favorites is The Forest Temple, which is the first dungeon you'll complete as adult Link. However, it's not as easy as it sounds, as the training wheels are completely off. So if you're struggling to beat The Forest Temple, want to ensure you don't miss any Gold Skulltulas, or just want an easy time solving all the puzzles, here's a complete walkthrough.

Upon entering the Forest Temple, you will be immediately attacked by two Wolfos. These are aggressive enemies that strike at you with a three-hit melee combo. The trick to killing them is to perform a backflip right before they attack. This will cause them to miss, spinning them around so they face away from Link and expose the glowing blue weak point on their spine.

Kill both of the Wolfos and look towards the door that's chained up with a lock on it. Introducing a new dungeon mechanic you'll need to become very familiar with. Going forward, some doors will have these chains and locks on them, which can only be removed if you find a Small Key. These keys are finite and will be consumed upon using them on a locked door!

Look to your right at the large tree with all the vines. Use these to climb to the top and carefully make your way across the flat tree branches to reach the small chest. This will contain a Small Key, which we will use to unlock the door to the actual Forest Temple.

Gold Skulltula 1 — Forest Temple Entrance (1F)

Our first Gold Skulla can be found at the Forest Temple entrance. At the very top of the tree covered in vines will be a Gold Skulltula. Kill it with the Hookshot and snag the token.

Go down the hallway, kill the Skulltula that drops from the ceiling, and enter the Grand Hall. When you arrive in Luigi's Mansion the Forest Temple, four different colored Poes will appear and steal the fire from the torches that match their colors. Each of them will run off to a corner of the dungeon, and your goal is to restore the fire they stole.

In the Grand Hall, make your way to the door at the top of the stairs directly across from where you entered.

Conveniently, your next Gold Skullatula can be found near the top of the stairs on the north side of the Grand Hall. At the top of the stairs, turn to your right, and you'll see the spider clinging to the wall. Like the previous one, use your Hookshot to kill and take the token it leaves behind.

Going through the next hall will introduce you to a new enemy called the Blue Bubble. This adorable name is given to the giant flaming skull with bat wings, which will attempt to ram itself into you. Use your shield to block the attack, extinguish its flames, and allow you to land an easy killing blow. Once it's dead, go into the next room.

Oh look, more new enemies. This time the door will lock behind you, and two Stalfos will appear. These are the most dangerous normal enemies you've encountered so far. Boasting a shield and sword, these skeletons can easily block your attacks and perform big sweeping strikes. The key to defeating them is to wait until one performs a jump attack and dodge it. This leaves them wide open to be hit, but it will take several sword slashes to bring one down.

When both Stalfos are dead, a small chest will appear with a Small Key inside. Head back the way you came and return to the Grand Hall.

In the Grand Hall, look for a door with a large blue block in front of it. Stand next to the block, pull out your ocarina, and play the Song of Time. This will cause the block to vanish, allowing you to use the door behind it. Go through the door and kill the Deku Babas while making your way over to the wall on the right.

Use the Hookshot to kill the two Skullwalltulas and climb up the vines to the balcony. Look to the wall with the large archways, and you'll see a Hookshot grapple point above one of the balconies. Pull yourself over to it with the Hookshot, kill the Blue Bubble, and step onto the switch to drain most of the water out of the well below you.

Jump down the well into the water and run down the path to the chest to get a second Small Key. Climb out of the well and be ready to deflect a projectile from the Octorok in the small fountain. Once it's dead, use the grapple point on the pillar to pull yourself over to the large chest. Open it to find the Forest Temple dungeon map. Exit out the door behind you and step on the switch to raise the gate, putting you back in the Grand Hall again.

Since we cannot use the Fairy Slingshot anymore, we need to find a suitable replacement. In the Grand Hall, make your way to the red-colored side of the room and up the stairs to the locked door. Use a Small Key to open it and head inside. Go down the hall and enter the next room where a Blue Bubble and a third new enemy, the Wallmaster, await.

As you run around, you'll notice a colored circle appear at Link's feet. Keep running back and forth until the large, severed hand lands on the ground. If it hits you, the Wallmaster will toss you back to the Grand Hall, so make sure to avoid it! Once the Wallmaster lands on the ground, turn and immediately kill it before the monster can retreat. When it's dead, kill the Blue Bubble and head up the ladder.

Now, go down the narrow passage and take the second left. Walk along the balcony until you reach a door with a large block in it. Push the block into the hallway you just ran down so it lines up with the door. Get behind the block and push it down the hall with the triangles on the floor. Once you reach the end of the hall, run along the balcony side again and shove the block a final time so it's under the opening above you.

Do not go up to the next floor; instead, go to where the block originally was and climb up the ladder along the wall. Follow the path until you find another block and push it forward into the adjoining hall. Go back down the ladder, run to where you finished placing the original block, and climb up. Go to the block you shoved into the hall and push it towards the second elevated door. Use it as makeshift stairs, then take a right and use the ladder.

Kill the two Blue Bubbles and then use your remaining Small Key to go through the locked door. Make your way down the twisted hall and quickly jump across the gap to the door on the right. There will be another Wallmaster, but with such minimal space to move, you're better off just avoiding it.

In the next room, there will be portraits of the Red Poe, but ignore them for now. Head down the stairs and through the door to trigger another fight with three Stalfos. I know I shouldn't have to say it, but just to make sure: do not stand in the glowing green poison pit in the middle. Upon killing all the skeleton warriors, a large chest will appear with the Fairy Bow inside.

With your newly acquired Fairy Bow, it's time to hunt down that Red Poe, Jo. Shoot the eye above the door you entered through and go back into the staircase. Use the bow to shoot any painting with the Red Poe in it, but keep your distance; otherwise, the ghost will flee. There will be one painting at the top of each staircase and a third over the bottom of the lower stairs.

After the ghost is forced out of the paintings, she will retreat to the lowest level of this room. Go down the stairs and draw your sword. Jo's main attack sees her spin like a top in front of you, deflecting all attacks. The trick is to turn away from her to bait this move out, then quickly run out of the way once she starts spinning. At the end of the move, Jo will be dizzy and vulnerable to your sword swings.

Do this twice to finally kill Jo and restore the red flame to the Grand Hall. Three more Poes left!

Open the small chest that Jo left behind to find a Small Key and head back into the room where you found the Fairy Bow. Use the key on the locked door directly ahead of you and go inside. This will bring you to another staircase room where Beth, the Blue Poe, is located. Just like with Jo, use the Fairy Bow to shoot the three portraits that specifically contain Beth in them.

This will make her retreat to the bottom of the stairs where you can fight her. Similar to Jo, Beth relies on a spin attack that makes her dizzy if she misses. The catch is, Beth will repeat the spin twice in succession, so make sure to dodge her second attack! Once she is recovering, run over to Beth and start slashing away.

Remember, you can goad Beth into attacking by looking away from her. When you do this, she will physically manifest herself, allowing you to turn and attack when she gets close. Upon killing Beth, the blue flame will return to the Grand Hall and a large chest will spawn containing the Compass.

With her dead, go back the way you came through both the Fairy Bow Room and where you killed Jo. Head down the twisted hallway and go through the door. When you exit the door at the end of the twisted hall, turn around and look over the door. Use the Fairy Bow to shoot the eye switch to reorient the hallway so it's normal.

Go down the previously twisted hall and jump down onto the lower floor. A Wallmaster will attempt to grab you, so make sure to dodge its attack and kill it. Once the Wallmaster is dead, open up the very ornate chest to find the Boss Key! Don't get ahead of yourself though; we have some work to do before battling the Forest Temple boss.

Jump down into the hole next to the chest, kill the two Blue Bubbles, and proceed through the door. You will emerge on a balcony overlooking the garden where you drained the well.

Gold Skulltula 3 — Second Floor Garden (1F)

On the second floor of the garden where you emptied the well, carefully walk out onto the ledge to the left of the door. Follow it around the corner until you see the Gold Skulltula in front of you. Use the Hookshot to kill and grab the token.

On the balcony, take the first door on the right to enter a small room. Inside will be a Floormaster, which is a troublesome enemy. It will launch itself at you, so block with your shield and strike it down. When it dies, the Floormaster will split into three smaller versions that are going to try to latch onto Link. If they do, the Floormaster will recover and grow to full size.

You prevent this by quickly killing all three mini-Floormasters before they can grab Link. A spin attack works wonders here since its large radius lets you hit multiple enemies at once! With the Floormaster defeated, open the chest to get a Small Key, head back onto the balcony, and go through the next door.

This will spit you out into the block room. Go back up to where the entrance to the twisted hall is and shoot the eye switch again. This will contort the hall back to its previous state, allowing you to reach where you killed Jo and Beth.

You can get a Purple Rupee in the block room on the second floor by shooting the long eye switch. This will drop a treasure chest containing your prize.

Head back to the top of the stairs where you killed Beth and go through the door. Go up the ladder on the right and run down the hall with the two Green Bubbles. Kill both of them and use your Small Key to unlock the next door. In this room, you will find a frozen eye switch and an unlit torch. Step on the stone switch to ignite the torch, and then fire your bow through the torch. This will set the arrow on fire, melting the ice around the eye switch.

Now shoot the eye switch to contort the blue hallway, go back down the hall, and jump down the hole in the floor to the lower level. In the checkered room, when you get close, the ceiling will begin to rise and fall. Ignore this for now and head to the right and through the door. Push the block onto the ground and then go back through the door to the checkered floor room.

Your final Gold Skulltula in the Forest Temple can be found in the West Garden. After you've pushed the large block down to make a shortcut, look above you at the small balcony to see a Scarecrow spawn point. Play the Scarecrow Song to summon him and then use the Hookshot to pull yourself onto the balcony. There you'll find the Gold Skulltula.

Wait until the floor falls onto the ground and then quickly run to the stone switch on the right. Be careful, as there are several Skulltulas that will fall from the ceiling if you get close, so be on the lookout for their shadows on the floor! Once you step on the switch to unlock the door, wait until the ceiling rises again and go to the small chest to get a few Bombs.

Now run to the door once it's safe and go through. In the Poe Room, shoot the large painting of Amy on the wall. She will then cause her image to become split up across five blocks. You need to push those blocks together to form her portrait once again. The catch is that one of the blocks that shows the right side of her head is fake. Look for the image of her head to occasionally move, as that signifies which block is real and which is fake.

Once the blocks are pushed together to form her image, Amy will appear and attack. Just like her sisters, Amy will perform a spin attack. Unlike them, she can do it three times in succession. Wait until she gets dizzy from all the spinning, and then attack. Repeat this a second time until Amy is dead, and the green flame is restored.

Go through the door to directly enter a hallway with a portrait of the Purple Poe on the wall. Shoot the painting to force the Poe out of hiding, causing her to retreat into the Grand Hall. Follow through the newly revealed door and jump down to where the torches are to initiate a fight with Meg, the final sister.

Unlike her siblings, Meg isn't content just spinning around like a top. Instead, she will clone herself and try to hide among her copies. Stand in the center of the room and look for a Meg that is holding a torch with a purple flame. Pull out your Fairy Bow and shoot this Poe to stun her and force her to vanish.

The next set of Megs will all have unlit torches, except the real one, which will still have a purple flame. Just like before, shoot the real Meg with an arrow to make her retreat. Her final set of clones will all look exactly the same and have the same colored torch. This time, you want to pay attention to her movements. As they circle around Link, only one of them will do a little spin.

When you see the spin, shoot that Meg with an arrow. If you're not fast enough, she will attack you and then hide back among her clones. The best way to quickly determine which one is real is by positioning the camera so it's looking directly down at you and the four Megs (shown above). Once you hit the real Meg with an arrow, strike her several times with your sword to finally kill her.

With all four Poes dead, take the elevator in the middle of the Grand Hall down to the lowest level of the Forest Temple. Grab the horizontal stone pillar on the wall and push it once. This will cause the walls to rotate around the room, revealing a small chamber with a stone switch on the floor. Step on the switch to ignite the torch.

Grab the stone pillar and push it again twice to reveal a room with a small chest that contains some arrows. Then push the pillar a third time to reveal a green-colored room with a few pots and a Skulltula. This will also line up the torch with the frozen eye switch. Shoot an arrow through the fire to thaw out the switch, and then hit it with an arrow to unlock the boss's room.

Gold Skulltula 5 — Outside The Boss Room (2B)

The final Gold Skulltula in the Forest Temple is located outside of the Boss Room. Rotate the room via the pillar a total of four times until the green-colored chamber with the pots is accessible. Go inside and look up at the wall to find the Gold Skulltula.

The big boss of the Forest Temple is Phantom Ganon, who is a fake version of the real man. When the fight begins, Phantom Ganon will retreat into one of the large paintings while on horseback. Equip your Fairy Bow and begin looking at all the paintings around you until you find two of them that show Phantom Ganon riding straight at Link.

One of these is real, and one is fake. You won't be able to tell who the phony is until he is about to escape the painting. Right before they reach the end of the road, the real one will continue pushing through the painting into the real world, while the fake turns around to ride back to the castle. The moment the real Ganon begins to appear in the boss room, shoot him with an arrow to force him back.

If you're too slow, he will jump over you and fire a projectile that deals a decent amount of damage. Repeat this a second time, making sure to shoot the real Phantom Ganon as you emerge from the painting. Where things get tricky is that the third time sees Phantom Ganon appearing in every painting.

Keep spinning your camera around once he gets close to the emerging to ensure you can see the real one as it escapes. Shoot it with an arrow to finally force Phantom Ganon off his horse. Put your bow away and draw your sword.

Phantom Ganon will float into the air, create a massive orb of energy, and hurl it at you. Instead of dodging, smack the orb when it's close to you with your sword to send it back to Phantom Ganon. He will do the same and return the orb to you. Keep volleying the energy orb back and forth until it eventually hits him. When this happens, rush over to Phantom Ganon and begin hacking away with your sword.

Learning absolutely nothing, Phantom Ganon will attempt this same attack again. Just like before, keep returning his serve until the orb strikes him and knocks the boss to the ground. Slash him a few more times before he retreats into the air. This time, Phantom Ganon will not just summon an orb, but will launch himself across the ground in an attempt to skewer you.

When he initiates the attack, dodge to the left or right to avoid this move. He may do this move multiple times in a row, so be ready! Eventually, he will summon the orb for you to smack back at him once again. This time, you should be able to kill him before he can recover, so slash away with your sword until the boss is defeated. After a brief cutscene, grab your Heart Container and step into the blue circle, completing the Forest Temple!

How To Complete Lord Jabu-Jabu's Belly In The Ocarina Of Time Remake

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time remake has finally been released, which means it's time to once again stop Ganondorf from taking over Hyrule. Throughout this adventure game, you will explore all manner of weird and thematic locations, but none are stranger than the inside of Lord Jabu-Jabu.

A massive fish worshiped by the Zora, Lord Jabu-Jabu is the game's third dungeon and can be one of the trickiest if you're a newcomer. So if you're struggling at all, need a refresher, or just want to make sure you don't miss any Gold Skulltulas, here's a full walkthrough of Lord Jabu-Jabu's Belly.

After being sucked in by Lord Jabu-Jabu, you will start in this fish's mouth. Before progressing, I recommend equipping the Deku Shield, as there's nothing to burn it up like in Dodongo's Cavern, and you can use it to reflect projectiles.

Speaking of, two Octoroks will attack you after taking a few steps forward. Use your shield to bounce their projectiles back at them, instantly killing these enemies. Now, equip your Fairy Sling Shot and shoot the glowing yellow switch on the ceiling to open the door ahead.

The next room will introduce you to another new enemy called Biri, which are electrified jellyfish. They will stun you if attacked while floating and are immune to your Deku Seeds. Because of this, you'll want to use a Deku Nut to stun and force them to the ground where they can be safely dispatched. Head through the next door when you're ready.

Upon entering, you will discover Princess Ruto, and a brief cutscene will trigger. After refusing your help, she will accidentally fall down a hole. Once you regain control of Link, jump down the hole and talk to her. She will concede to letting you help her, which means you get to carry her around!

Why aren't you excited about this? It's a great honor, or so I'm told.

Our first goal is to unlock the Dungeon Map. Grab Ruto and head through the door directly next to her. The next hall will be filled with bubble enemies called Shaboms, which are relatively harmless unless you touch them. Either run past or kill all of them before entering the next room.

Jump into the large empty pit with Ruto and toss her onto the ledge on the other side of the room. Step on the glowing yellow switch to raise the water level and swim over to the large chest on the left. Inside will be the Dungeon Map! Be careful, as two enemies known as Stingers will spawn. They are quite aggressive and can be killed via hitting them twice with the Fairy Slingshot.

Your first Gold Skulltula in Lord Jabu-Jabu can be found in the Dungeon Map room. After you raise the water, swim over to the vines directly across from the large chest and climb up to the elevated area. Kill the Gold Skulltula clinging to the wall to claim your prize.

Now that we have the map, rejoin Ruta by swimming over to the side you threw her up to, but don't pick her up just yet. Step up to the next hall to cause a Bari to fall from the ceiling. Just like the Biri, they are quite dangerous and cannot be directly attacked without taking damage. Instead, use a Deku Nut for now to stun it and give you an opening to strike. Be careful: when a Bari dies, it will split into three Biri!

At the end of the hall, there's another ceiling switch you have to shoot with the Fairy Sling to open. In the next room there will be an Octorok, so kill it and step on the switch to activate an elevator. This will take you back to the throat, so go through the door and bring Ruta to the main chamber where you initially met.

In the room where you activated the elevator, there's an underwater tunnel you can swim through that leads to a Business Scrub. Use this to resupply if you need any items!

Now that we're back where we started, take Ruto across the room and go through the door directly ahead of you (this will be north on your map). Be careful running across to the other side, as there are multiple Bari and Biri that will drop from the ceiling. Additionally, you'll want to avoid all the large pink circles on the ground, as these will open up and drop you to the floor below.

In the subsequent room, step on the switch directly to your left and bring Ruto into the room that unlocks. Set her down once the door locks behind you before engaging the four Stinger that emerge from the ground. Play defensively and use your shield to block when they charge at you. Once they hit the shield, strike them twice with the sword to land a killing blow.

When all four Stingers are defeated, a large chest will spawn with the Boomerang inside. This will be your main tool throughout the entire dungeon, so toss it around the room a few times to get used to how it moves. When you're ready, grab Ruto and head back out into what we will call the Connection Room.

Head back out into the Connection Room and use your newly acquired item to kill both of the Tailparsan in the room. When they're dead, go right and set Ruto down on the switch that's below the large pink tentacle. This will keep the door unlocked, allowing you to enter. Inside, there will be a large pink parasitic tentacle. Lock onto it with Z-Targeting and throw the Boomerang at the thinner section in the middle.

This will cause it to take damage, so quickly start strafing in a circle around the room to avoid its counterattack. Once it's done swinging, throw the Boomerang at it again. Repeat this process a couple more times until the tentacle is destroyed. Head back outside, pick up Ruto, and follow the blue tentacle down the hallway you just opened up.

In the next room will be the blue parasitic tentacle and a few Biri. Enter the room and focus on dispatching the Biri first, as they can be a real nuisance when fighting the tentacle. Do not stop moving once you start fighting, as the tentacle is relentless in its attacks and is surprisingly fast despite its size.

When all the Biri are dead, wait until the tentacle runs out of steam and rests on the ground for a couple of seconds. Use this opening to hit it with the Boomerang before resuming dodging its attacks. Keep this up until the tentacle is dead, pick Ruto up, and head back into the Connection Room once more.

Go down the hall and follow the green tentacle this time into the next room. Just like the prior two tentacles, deal with the enemies guarding it before shifting your focus to the main threat. When the green tentacle is dead, take Ruto out and go to the room directly next to the one you just left. Inside, there will be a ton of Shaboms, so use the Boomerang to kill all of them to cause a large chest to spawn that contains the Compass.

Follow the dissolving green tentacle back to the main room where you met Ruta. A brief cutscene will play that shows a new route opening up with the tentacle's destruction. Use the Boomerang to kill the Bari and Biri that drop from the ceiling and then fall through the hole that has just opened up.

Gold Skulltula 2 & 3 — Main Room, Lower Floor (B1)

After you drop down the hole, you open up in the main chamber, you will find one Gold Skulltula on the northern wall above you and another in the hall leading northwest. Use your Boomerang to kill both, then throw the Boomerang at the token to bring it back to Link!

With the Gold Skulltulas dead, take Ruto and head into the next room to the northwest. This will trigger a brief cutscene where she sees the Spiritual Stone of Water in the center of the room. Toss Ruto up to the stone, which activates the elevator she's on, taking her up to the floor above you. Unfortunately for you, a Big Octo will ride the elevator back down and attack.

This is your first real dungeon mini-boss, a theme that will appear in all future dungeons you explore. When the fight starts, equip your Boomerang and throw it at the Big Octo to stun him. This will cause him to become immobile and stop chasing you in a circle around the arena. Quickly run behind him and smack the green blob on his backside to damage Big Octo.

If you're not fast enough, don't worry; stay behind him and throw your Boomerang again to stun Big Octo and give you an opening to attack. After you successfully damage him, Big Octo will pop up on the other side of the arena and spawn several Shaboms. Focus on the Shaboms, as you have limited space to move, so it's really easy to accidentally run into them.

Continue using your Boomerang to stun Big Octo so you can slash at his weak point with your sword until he is defeated. So long as you manage the Shaboms and keep your distance from this mini-boss, you should have no trouble beating this enemy. When he's dead, step onto the elevator Ruto took and go upstairs.

With Ruto gone, we need to find where she has run off to since she has the Spiritual Stone of Water. After you take the elevator, go through the door and approach the two platforms in the room. They will come to life and damage anyone who gets close to them. Quickly use your Boomerang to stun both of them, allowing you to safely cross the room without being hit.

In the next room, you will see a floor switch that requires someone (or something) to be on it to keep the door unlocked. Since we don't have Ruto anymore, look to your right to see a small stone box. Run over, grab the box, and toss it up to the upper level where the switch is. Now climb up the vines and place the box onto the switch to unlock the door.

The next room has several Biri in it and a large column on the right side. Kill the Biri with your Boomerang and use the vines to climb up to the top of the column.

Gold Skulltula 4 — Outside The Boss Room (2F)

You can find the last Gold Skulltula in Lord Jabu-Jabu right outside of the boss room where the large column is. After defeating the Biri, you can find the spider clinging to the vines on the column, so use your Boomerang or Fairy Slingshot to kill this enemy. Now just climb up and claim your token.

While on the column, look towards the northwest wall to see a yellow switch behind the wall. Throw your Boomerang through either the right or left hole to curve it around the wall and strike the switch. This will cause the door to open, allowing you into the next room. The final room before the boss fight has no enemies and just some pots. Make sure you have your Boomerang equipped before going inside!

Introducing Barinade, the main boss of this dungeon. Unlike the previous two dungeon bosses, fighting Barinade is mostly about just whittling away at its defenses to expose the vulnerable core body. Barinade is also the most dangerous foe you've faced so far, as it has multiple attacks, many of which can strike you at range.

When the fight begins, keep your distance and equip the Boomerang. Barinade will begin firing laser beams from its upper tentacles, so staying on the move at all times will ensure that it always misses. Just like the three colored parasitic tentacles you encountered before, your first goal is to destroy the tentacles attached to the ceiling.

This is done by hitting the exposed, white colored sections of the tentacles with your Boomerang. You can do this by either locking onto to each tentacle and throwing your weapon at it or just manually aiming the Boomerang by holding down the left trigger. I personally prefer the latter since it's easier to hit them while on the move.

Once all three tentacles have been severed, Barinade will take exception to this and launch several large, electrified jellyfish around it. These will begin spinning around the main body and are completely immune while their electrified shields are active. Keep your distance and focus on avoiding the spinning jellyfish for now. Eventually, Barinade will tire itself out, and all the shields on the jellyfish will vanish.

Seize this opportunity and destroy as many of them as you can with your Boomerang. You can use your sword for this; however, there's a chance of the jellyfish shocking you if their shield activates while you are attacking them! Keep in mind, once the jellyfish lose their shields, Barinade will start firing its lasers at you again, so stay on the move while attacking!

After all the jellyfish are dead, Barinade will rip itself from the ground and begin charging at you at a high speed. It will be completely invulnerable while charging, so just run away from it to ensure you aren't hit. Once Barinade slows down, throw the Boomerang at the main, glowing orange body of the boss. This will stun it, causing the shields on the jellyfish to vanish. Just like before, take out as many jellyfish as you can before it resumes attacking.

With all the jellyfish destroyed, Barinade will finally be vulnerable to your attacks. In a final, desperate attempt to defeat you, Barinade will begin spinning around the room and firing lasers. Hit the main, pink body of Barinade with your Boomerang to stun it, causing the boss to fall to the floor.

Now run over to Barinade and start whacking away with your sword until it recovers. Back up and use the Boomerang again to stun the boss, allowing you to strike it once more. Keep repeating this until Barinade is dead, signaling your completion of the Lord Jabu-Jabu's Belly dungeon!

Link is the iconic protagonist from the Legend of Zelda. But not all versions of the green tunic-wearing warrior are equal in power.

We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.

DoorDash Just Bought The Ad Business It Couldn’t Build For Itself

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Last week, the news became official: DoorDash acquired Instacart for $17 billion.

Earlier in the week, when the deal was still a well-sourced rumor, Needham, an investment bank, put out a note regarding the deal. Needham’s working theory was that the deal is about combining the two advertising businesses. Everything else is logistics.

So what does this deal actually mean for commerce media?

The easy read is that a delivery app is buying another delivery app. Actually, DoorDash is buying a revenue mix it has never managed to build for itself. Not for lack of trying. DoorDash bought ad-tech company Symbiosys for $175 million in 2025 to take its ads beyond its own app, and it has spent years pushing into grocery and retail.

Buying Instacart is DoorDash’s admission that building on its own wasn’t getting there fast enough.

Here is the arithmetic. Instacart’s advertising and other revenue was $297 million in the second quarter, 28.5% of everything it earned. Call it more than a quarter of the company. DoorDash passed a $1 billion run rate in ads two years ago and hasn’t broken the number out since. By my count, that’s around 10% of its revenue.

Both companies have billion-dollar ad businesses. Only one of them is an ad business.

That matters because delivery is a hard business and advertising is a highly profitable one. According to Wedbush, a financial securities firm, Instacart brings more than grocery drop-offs; it brings a marketplace across many grocers, plus an ad business brands pay to rank higher in search.

Delivery is low margin, and ads are high margin. Ads lift what the platform earns per order without charging the customer more. That is the beauty of a blended P&L.

How did Instacart end up setting the bar? It became an ads business because the grocers couldn’t deliver what they had promised brands.

Retail media was pitched as performance media for commerce. Ads at the point of conversion, closed loop, every dollar accounted for. What brands got from most grocers was run-of-site advertising with a sales report attached.

Some grocers built serious networks, such as Kroger Precision Marketing and Albertsons Media Collective. But even the good ones could only reach one retailer’s shoppers, and that left brands stitching together a dozen buys to cover a category.

Instacart could do something the grocers couldn’t. Because it sat on top of the last mile, it could bundle baskets and get hyper-specific across hundreds of retailers in one buy. A brand didn’t have to negotiate with Kroger, then Costco, then Publix. It bought the category once.

That’s the Uber model – a network of independent operators behind one front door – applied to grocery. (Not so coincidentally, Uber was also reportedly in the mix to acquire Instacart, but it backed out.)

Together, DoorDash and Instacart reach across occasions no one else covers in one place. DoorDash’s share of US delivery spending has grown from 44% to 59% over five years, while Instacart’s fell from 26% to 18%. Combined, a CPG brand could find the same household ordering dinner on DoorDash on Tuesday and groceries on Instacart on Saturday, across restaurants, grocery and convenience in a single buy.

But Instacart has differentiators that could prove to be worth its $17 billion price tag. Instacart is not reliant on individual grocery retailers, because it has direct relationships with consumers. Consumers opt in to sharing loyalty card data with Instacart for rewards at the grocer, and Instacart carries that opt-in. Plus, Instacart operates its own planogram model, so it decides how prominently it features products based on its own brand deals.

All of those factors make Instacart a true commerce media business. And Instacart’s direct relationships with consumers and brands can’t be easily disrupted by a grocer deciding to launch its own retail media platform.

Meanwhile, DoorDash built its relationships directly with restaurants. So Instacart’s relationships with grocers are truly additive.

Now, here’s the uncomfortable part: attribution.

Instacart nailed attribution on its own app. It has a 14-day window, basket-level reporting, a clean room since January and MRC accreditation for its impressions and clicks. What it never nailed is attribution to the retailer’s total sales, which is what the grocers promised in the first place.

Instacart sees the Instacart basket. Most grocery still happens in a store, and that halo is modeled, not measured. Brands kept spending anyway. They pay for the basket, not the proof. What is interesting is that the burden of proof shifted to the retailer rather than Instacart. Instacart can prove what happens in its own basket. So when a brand asks what its spend did in the store, the retailer is the one left without an answer.

There’s a bigger point here for commerce media. We’ve spent five years calling it an emerging channel, the thing on the test budget. It has just been priced as a scaled business asset, with closed-loop measurement on its own basket and modeling to show brands its impact in-store.

A public company just paid $17 billion, about a 30% premium, largely for an ad business built on shopper data. And the analysts covering the deal are valuing that ad business, not the drivers.

Commerce media is no longer just a line in the marketing plan; it is a line in the acquisition model.

“Data-Driven Thinking” is written by members of the media community and contains fresh ideas on the digital revolution in media.

Follow Kristina Kaganer, Auxbow and AdExchanger on LinkedIn.

High-end ad blockers mimic some of the same behavior as bot traffic. So WiseGuyGaming repurposed the tools it was already using to identify bots to reveal its ad-blocking audience as well.

Criteo has weathered considerable storms in its day, including a painful moment in March 2020 when its market cap bottomed out below $500 million. Can it pull off yet another turnaround?

Disaffection and outright anger toward US sports institutions run deeper than logo-cluttered fields. Leagues and teams are sacrificing player safety and fan enjoyment for marketing dollars.

People Inc.’s referral traffic from Google search is down 70% over the past two years, and by 26% in Q3 alone. But its digital revenue is still growing, albeit slowly.

On Tuesday, Chord, a six-year-old commerce tech startup, acquired Zenlytic, an AI startup with an anthropomorphized data analyst agent named “Zoë,” for $5 million.

If you ask the executive leadership of Affle and Perion why the two companies are merging, the answer you’ll get is “synergies.”

AdExchanger is where marketers, agencies, publishers and tech companies go for the latest information on the trends that are transforming digital media and marketing, from data, privacy, identity and AI to commerce, CTV, measurement and mobile.
